    ci: compile the instrumented tests
    
    androidTest was never built in CI, so when #56 replaced addFriend(uid) with
    the request/accept flow, FirestoreFriendRepositoryTest kept calling the old
    method and stopped compiling. A source set that does not compile takes every
    test in it down, so Room, DataStore and the safety worker tests were
    unrunnable too -- and nothing said so, because assembleDebug and
    testDebugUnitTest both pass straight through a broken androidTest.
    
    Compiling is as far as this job can go: actually running them needs a device
    plus the Firestore and Auth emulators. That is still enough to catch the
    failure that happened. Verified by putting the addFriend call back and
    watching the new step fail with the same three unresolved references.
    
    No secrets needed: the TEST_ACCOUNT_* build config fields default to empty
    strings, and only GOOGLE_WEB_CLIENT_ID hard-fails the build, which CI already
    supplies.

    this app's theme defines -- so it rendered in stock Material purple and read
    as a screen from a different app. It is now RepMateCard rows, surfaceVariant

    the app puts it, and a stateless FriendsContent with previews.

    SessionDetailScreen. Those two copies are now one RepMateTopBar so a fourth
    screen cannot drift; both screens render pixel-identically to before.

      which reads as a real (terrible) score. Abandoned sessions now show a
      muted "-".
    - Home said "leaderboard, today" next to an all-time figure.
    - observeFriends() resolves each friend's live users/{uid}.displayName
      instead of trusting the name copied into the friendship document, so the
      same person no longer appears as "RepMate User" on Friends and their real
      name on the leaderboard.

commit ae9abd45a0181a826ddb604bfd440d847dd50a5c
Author:     Mohit Nanda <mohit240903@gmail.com>
AuthorDate: Thu Oct 8 20:20:02 2026 +1100
Commit:     Mohit Nanda <mohit240903@gmail.com>
CommitDate: Thu Oct 8 20:20:02 2026 +1100

    fix(test): rewrite the friend repository test for the request/accept flow
    
    The instrumented source set has not compiled since #56 removed
    FriendRepository.addFriend: FirestoreFriendRepositoryTest still called it in
    six places. Because a source set either compiles or it does not, that took
    every instrumented test in the module down with it -- Room, DataStore, the
    SessionDao queries, the safety worker, 46 tests in total, none of them
    runnable. CI never compiles androidTest, so nothing reported it.
    
    The tests are rewritten against the flow that replaced addFriend: a request
    is sent, and the recipient accepts or rejects it. What they now cover:
    
    - accepting creates the friendship for BOTH users, including the half the
      accepting user has to write on the sender's behalf
    - accepting consumes the request
    - rejecting removes the request and creates no friendship
    - a pending request is visible to the recipient and not to the sender
    - removing a friend deletes both sides, so nobody is left with a one-sided
      friendship
    - a request for a name nobody has, and a blank name, are refused
    - one user still cannot read another's friend list
    
    The request document is written as the sender rather than through
    sendFriendRequest, because that method resolves the recipient through a
    usernames/{name} claim and claiming a name means writing the claim and the
    profile in one rules-satisfying batch. Seeding that would make these tests
    mostly about name claims. Writing the request as the sender is exactly what
    the rules allow, so the accept path under test gets the same document it would
    in the app. The class comment says so, and says what the file needs to run.
    
    Verified: compileDebugAndroidTestKotlin succeeds, and 30 instrumented tests
    now run on a Pixel 10a (RoomGuestSessionStore, SessionDao, SessionDaoBestScore,
    RoomSessionRepository, DataStorePendingMigrationStore) -- all passing, the
    first time any instrumented test has been runnable since #56.
    
    Not verified: these eight tests themselves. They need the Firestore and Auth
    emulators plus TEST_ACCOUNT_A/B credentials, and port 8080 on this machine is
    held by an unrelated service. Whoever owns the friends feature should run them
    once before relying on them.

commit 375ab3e38795b1f52a6a23be1b6cf7ed9c3cebde
Author:     Mohit Nanda <mohit240903@gmail.com>
AuthorDate: Thu Oct 8 19:53:39 2026 +1100
Commit:     Mohit Nanda <mohit240903@gmail.com>
CommitDate: Thu Oct 8 19:53:39 2026 +1100

    feat(pushup): report a finished push-up set from its elbow angle
    
    Push-ups had no end-of-set curve, and could not get one the way squats and
    jumping jacks do: they are counted from the camera, and the accelerometer
    frames recorded alongside describe a phone propped up and deliberately held
    still, so there is no movement in them to replay. Their movement is the elbow
    angle, which is not a MotionFrame and never enters the engine's frame
    pipeline.
    
    So the angle is collected as the set happens. PushupAngleTrace keeps each
    counted rep's smoothed elbow angle, cutting reps where the detector does --
    from the frame the arm first reads as bent to the frame that completes the rep
    -- and dropping the setup and rest frames between them. It is pure Kotlin and
    driven entirely by what it is handed, so it runs in a plain JVM test.
    
    The report reuses what already exists rather than adding a second chart: a
    push-up rep is a curve against a band, exactly like a calibrated IMU rep, so
    AccelerometerCurveChart took two caption parameters instead. The band is the
    detector's own straight/bent pair (126.5/152.6), which is what the rep had to
    achieve to count at all -- the same role the calibration band plays for the
    IMU exercises.
    
    - PushupFrameProcessor.Result now reports smoothedDegrees, the value the
      detector decided on rather than the raw angle.
    - JustFinishedSessionStore gains a second slot for the curves, cleared with
      everything else when the next set starts. Nothing is persisted: Room stores
      no curve for a push-up any more than it stores frames for a squat.
    - The stale KDoc on the calibrated replay body ("not reachable yet for a real
      live session") is corrected; it has been reachable since the previous commit.
    
    16 new tests (396 JVM, 0 failures): 9 for the trace (where a rep's window
    starts and stops, unreadable frames skipped, a rep left open not reported, the
    per-rep cap, reset) and 7 for the report mapper (band, pairing each curve with
    its own rep's score, depth carried through, no traces means no report).
    Mutation-checked: 9 regressions, all 9 caught, including pairing curves with
    the wrong rep's score and inventing a score for an unpaired curve.
    
    Based on the jumping-jack commit, so this branch carries #59 as well --
    merge that first.

commit 04b645bce965153a454b477180734a87420f2efc
Author:     Mohit Nanda <mohit240903@gmail.com>
AuthorDate: Thu Oct 8 19:43:24 2026 +1100
Commit:     Mohit Nanda <mohit240903@gmail.com>
CommitDate: Thu Oct 8 19:43:24 2026 +1100

    feat(replay): replay jumping jacks too, not just squats
    
    SessionReplayer hardcoded SquatRepDetector, which was the only reason the
    replay was squat-only. Both IMU detectors expose the same smoothed
    acceleration magnitude -- JumpingJackRepDetector reuses the squat detector's
    250 ms filter and 10.15/9.7 trigger, as its own KDoc says -- so one curve
    shape serves both, and FormScorer already scores jacks live with no exercise
    branch.
    
    replay() now picks the detector that counted the set, closing over one
    instance the way LiveWorkoutViewModel.bindDetector does, so the two accessors
    can never refer to different exercises. A replayed jack set therefore
    re-derives exactly the reps the live detector counted. Its rep window spans a
    pair of impacts, so the curve shows two spikes where a squat shows one dip and
    rise: that is the movement, not a defect.
    
    The squat-only gates are gone. JustFinishedSessionStore.canReplay is now the
    single place that decides which exercises can be replayed, and the frame
    capture, the store and the replayer all read it, so they cannot disagree.
    
    Push-ups still return null and keep the no-replay notice: they are counted
    from the camera and the phone is deliberately held still throughout, so the
    accelerometer frames recorded alongside hold no movement. A push-up report
    needs the elbow-angle stream and a different chart; that is separate work.
    
    CLAUDE.md now says replay covers the two IMU exercises and why push-ups do
    not.
    
    5 new tests (380 JVM, 0 failures): a jack set replays with the jack detector
    and matches detecting directly, the two detectors provably disagree on that
    trace so the dispatch test cannot pass trivially, a push-up session is not
    replayed, and the store keeps both IMU exercises while refusing push-ups.
    Mutation-checked: 4 regressions (jacks through the squat detector, push-ups
    replayed, the store accepting push-ups, the store back to squats only), all 4
    caught.

commit c02e118d87e623b805c9336b437e284ee8c28caf
Author:     Mohit Nanda <mohit240903@gmail.com>
AuthorDate: Thu Oct 8 19:09:14 2026 +1100
Commit:     Mohit Nanda <mohit240903@gmail.com>
CommitDate: Thu Oct 8 19:09:14 2026 +1100

    feat(leaderboard): give both tabs real data
    
    Nothing ever wrote the leaderboard collection, so both tabs and Home's top
    three were permanently empty. The read path, queries, UI and empty states were
    all built and working; the producer was simply missing.
    
    Global tab: a real producer. LeaderboardSync recomputes the signed-in user's
    points from local history after a workout is saved and after History restores
    sessions, and FirestoreLeaderboardWriter upserts leaderboard/{uid} with the
    display name read from users/{uid}. The existing read path is untouched: it
    always queried for displayName and points.
    
    Friends tab: read from ghostScores instead, which Ghost Duel has been writing
    since #48/#57. Four single gets per user (one per exercise, one for the name),
    because ghostScores has allow list: if false by design. This tab therefore
    needs no rules change at all, so if the new write rule is not deployed the
    Friends tab still shows real scores while Global stays empty.
    
    Points are defined in one place, LeaderboardPoints: per exercise, the best
    session's average rep score times its rep count, summed. Quality alone would
    rank three careful reps above thirty good ones, and reps alone would reward
    flailing. Both tabs use the same function, so they cannot rank the same person
    differently.
    
    Also fixed, as agreed:
    - The nested collect in LeaderboardViewModel. The inner flow never completes,
      so the outer lambda never returned and friends-list changes were dropped:
      adding a second friend showed nothing until the screen was reopened. Now
      flatMapLatest.
    - The signed-in user is included in the friends query, so they can see their
      own standing.
    - The whereIn 30-item cap is gone with the per-user reads, and documented so
      nobody reintroduces a batched query without chunking.
    
    RULES CHANGE REQUIRED, NOT YET DEPLOYED: leaderboard/{userId} gains
    create/update/delete for the owner only, validating exactly displayName,
    points and updatedAt, with points a non-negative int under 100000 and
    updatedAt == request.time. Until it is deployed every write fails with
    PERMISSION_DENIED, which is logged and cannot break a workout save. 8 new
    emulator tests cover it.
    
    28 new JVM tests (397 total, 0 failures) and 8 new rules tests (67 total).
    Mutation-checked: 11 deliberate regressions, all 11 caught, including
    restoring the old nested collect to prove the refresh test catches it.

commit 3419cd646337a063c26ff1c273097e69ca6d1ebe
Author:     Mohit Nanda <mohit240903@gmail.com>
AuthorDate: Thu Oct 8 08:03:26 2026 +1100
Commit:     Mohit Nanda <mohit240903@gmail.com>
CommitDate: Thu Oct 8 08:03:26 2026 +1100

    feat(replay): replay the squat set the user just finished
    
    Motion Replay has never drawn a curve. Room has no column for MotionFrames
    and every save sets frames = null, so MotionReplayViewModel took its
    "no replay data" branch for every session, every time -- a built, tested
    screen with nothing to show.
    
    The frames now survive the end of a set in memory instead of being thrown
    away. JustFinishedSessionStore holds one session, LiveWorkoutViewModel fills
    it when a set ends, and MotionReplayViewModel prefers it over the Room load.
    Room still stores frames = null: no schema change, no migration, no sensor
    data persisted.
    
    Deliberately squat-only:
    - SessionReplayer re-derives rep windows with SquatRepDetector specifically,
      so a jumping-jack set would be cut up by the wrong detector.
    - A push-up set carries no body motion at all -- the phone is propped up and
      the stability gate requires it to stay still -- and push-ups are never
      calibrated, so there is no band either.
    Both keep the honest no-replay notice, and the store enforces this rather
    than trusting callers.
    
    Frames are captured at exactly the point they reach the detector, so
    replaying them with the same detector and profile re-derives the same reps:
    the replay cannot disagree with the rep count the workout screen showed.
    Capture is capped at 30,000 frames (ten minutes at ~50 Hz, roughly 2 MB) and
    stops there rather than dropping the oldest, so the replay's rep 1 is the
    set's rep 1. The store is cleared when the next workout starts.
    
    CLAUDE.md promised a motion-replay graph for the app generally, which was
    false in two directions: no session replayed at all, and the design only ever
    supported squats. It now says what the app does.
    
    7 new tests, mutation-checked: 5 deliberate regressions (ignoring the store,
    matching any session id, accepting non-squat or frame-less sessions, a clear()
    that does not clear), all 5 caught. 376 JVM tests, 0 failures.

commit e8dcb2ba09ae2e21ddb04ea7d29ea0800edaee60
Author:     Mohit Nanda <mohit240903@gmail.com>
AuthorDate: Sat Oct 3 15:03:34 2026 +1000
Commit:     Mohit Nanda <mohit240903@gmail.com>
CommitDate: Sat Oct 3 15:03:34 2026 +1000

    fix(sync): keep every pending guest merge, and test the real implementations
    
    A second guest session used to overwrite the first guest's pending marker
    while that guest's rows were still in Room under a UID nobody held any
    more, so nothing could ever find them again. PendingMigrationStore now
    holds a set keyed by guest UID instead of one slot:
    
    - markPending adds an entry and leaves an existing one for that guest
      alone, so a retried sign-in cannot wipe a recorded target
    - discardUnresolvedCapture takes the guest it is discarding, so a failed
      sign-in cannot drop a capture made before it
    - recordSignIn resolves every unresolved entry to the signed-in account
    - resume walks the whole queue, and one entry that keeps failing to
      upload no longer strands the others
    - DataStorePendingMigrationStore still reads the old single-marker keys,
      so an app update landing mid-merge does not orphan that guest either
    
    Tests for the parts that previously only had fakes behind them:
    
    - RoomGuestSessionStoreTest (instrumented, real DB): reassignOwner moves
      only rows still owned by the guest, leaves another account's row alone,
      reports 0 moved on a retry, carries rep scores across, and handles a
      history longer than one 500-id chunk
    - DataStorePendingMigrationStoreTest (instrumented, real DataStore): a
      pending merge survives into a new store instance; two guests both kept
    - FirestoreMigrationUploaderTest: a target that is not the signed-in user
      is refused and writes nothing at all
    - GuestMigrationLauncherTest: the app-start wait outlasts Firebase's
      initial signed-out state and times out rather than hanging; a failed
      merge step is reported but never breaks sign-in, while cancellation is
      not swallowed
    - GuestAuthRouteTest: a guest signing up is linked (UID preserved) and
      only logging in to an existing account captures for a merge
    - PendingMigrationCodecTest: round-trip, corrupt records skipped, and the
      pre-upgrade marker folded in ahead of newer captures
    
    To make the uploader testable without FirebaseFirestore, its one call is
    now behind a WorkoutUploader interface that FirestoreWorkoutDataSource
    implements. AuthViewModel's link-or-replace decision moved into
    guestAuthRoute so it can be asserted directly.
    
    320 JVM tests and 12 new instrumented tests pass. Every new test was
    mutation-checked: 8 deliberate regressions, all caught.
    
    firestore.rules is deliberately untouched: the merge reads the Room copy
    precisely so no rule has to be widened to reach a departed guest's
    documents.

commit 348f72eee64b60326eeea105bfbec6ef5507182c
Author:     Xue Li (Claire) <xli3326@student.unimelb.edu.au>
AuthorDate: Thu Oct 1 16:45:19 2026 +1000
Commit:     Xue Li (Claire) <xli3326@student.unimelb.edu.au>
CommitDate: Thu Oct 1 16:45:19 2026 +1000

    Add real depth-based scoring for push-ups
    
    Push-ups previously recorded a neutral placeholder RepScore (fixed 10f)
    for every rep since they use camera-based pose detection instead of IMU
    sensors and skip calibration entirely. This replaces that placeholder
    with real scoring based on how each rep's depth (elbow angle minimum)
    compares to the best depth reached so far in the session, combined with
    the existing tempo and consistency scoring approach used by FormScorer.
    
    - PushupRepDetector: track and report the bottom-of-rep angle for each
      completed rep via Transition.bottomDegrees
    - PushupFrameProcessor: thread repBottomDegrees through to the UI layer
    - PushupWorkoutViewModel: score each rep against the session's own best
      depth so far, falling back to NOT_MEASURABLE when depth can't be read
    
    Added two new unit tests for the depth-tracking logic in
    PushupRepDetectorTest. All 233 existing tests still pass.

 .../java/com/repmate/engine/PushupRepDetector.kt   |  15 ++-
 .../ui/workout/pushup/PushupFrameProcessor.kt      |   8 +-
 .../ui/workout/pushup/PushupWorkoutViewModel.kt    | 105 ++++++++++++++++-----
 .../com/repmate/engine/PushupRepDetectorTest.kt    |  21 +++++
 4 files changed, 121 insertions(+), 28 deletions(-)

    fix(manifest): add ACCESS_COARSE_LOCATION and telephony uses-feature for safety check-in
    
    - Adds ACCESS_COARSE_LOCATION paired with ACCESS_FINE_LOCATION to satisfy Android 12+ location lint rules.

    - Confirmed manifest labels contain no pushup typos.
    - Verified gradlew lintDebug and gradlew assembleDebug testDebugUnitTest pass.

commit 7df01039e3013139e07d64c3346329e79457ed50
Author:     Mohit Nanda <mohit240903@gmail.com>
AuthorDate: Tue Sep 29 00:22:52 2026 +1000
Commit:     Mohit Nanda <mohit240903@gmail.com>
CommitDate: Tue Sep 29 00:22:52 2026 +1000

    feat(ui): shared KeepScreenOn for every exercise screen
    
    The screen-on flag only existed as a private helper on the push-up screen, so
    squat, jumping-jack and calibration screens let the phone time out mid-set.
    Once the screen turns off Android stops delivering sensor events to the app,
    so pocketed IMU sets would silently stop counting.
    
    KeepScreenOn() is now shared and used by the push-up, live workout and
    calibration screens. Holds are counted so that navigating calibration ->
    workout, where the outgoing screen disposes after the incoming one starts,
    does not clear the flag under the screen that still needs it.

 .../java/com/repmate/ui/components/KeepScreenOn.kt | 70 ++++++++++++++++++++++
 .../com/repmate/ui/workout/LiveWorkoutScreen.kt    |  5 ++
 .../ui/workout/pushup/PushupWorkoutScreen.kt       | 15 +----
 .../com/repmate/ui/components/HoldCounterTest.kt   | 32 ++++++++++
 5 files changed, 114 insertions(+), 13 deletions(-)

commit b5c5baa7252f5c4fd40c68a587fba61430b34d1d
Author:     Mohit Nanda <mohit240903@gmail.com>
AuthorDate: Tue Sep 29 00:17:18 2026 +1000
Commit:     Mohit Nanda <mohit240903@gmail.com>
CommitDate: Tue Sep 29 00:17:18 2026 +1000

    fix(pushup): motion readout and log never fired (Long.MIN_VALUE timestamp overflow)
    
    frame.tMillis - Long.MIN_VALUE overflows to a negative number, so the 200 ms
    readout throttle and the 1 s motion log were never due. Found on a Pixel 10a:
    no "motion:" lines in logcat and no debug readout on screen. Extracted a
    Throttle that treats the first call as ready, with a test that fails on the
    old behaviour.

commit 06e2790084a580d992c9687377a0342a72ba9f48
Author:     Mohit Nanda <mohit240903@gmail.com>
AuthorDate: Tue Sep 29 00:09:01 2026 +1000
Commit:     Mohit Nanda <mohit240903@gmail.com>
CommitDate: Tue Sep 29 00:09:01 2026 +1000

    feat(pushup): gate counting on phone stability and a confident person, add front camera
    
    Reps are only fed to PushupRepDetector while the phone is steady (gyro/accel
    gate on the existing SensorSource), a person is tracked with confident arm
    landmarks, and an arm is locked. Each blocked state shows its own hint. A
    flip button switches between rear and front camera, keeping the rep count.
    The detector in com.repmate.engine is unchanged.
    
    Stability limits are untuned first proposals; debug builds show a live
    readout with tighter/looser buttons to tune them on a phone.

commit c54a2bc1ed93e313f8c08a2eceb45656fa1ba506
Author:     Mohit Nanda <mohit240903@gmail.com>
AuthorDate: Tue Sep 22 23:25:37 2026 +1000
Commit:     Mohit Nanda <mohit240903@gmail.com>
CommitDate: Tue Sep 22 23:25:37 2026 +1000

    feat(safety): add opt-in safety check-in after a workout
    
    Wires up the previously display-only toggle and Emergency contact row in
    Profile. When enabled, ending a workout schedules a WorkManager chain that
    posts an "Are you okay?" notification after 5 minutes, then texts the saved
    emergency contact (with a maps link to the last known location, if available)
    after a further 5 minutes unless "I'm OK" is tapped first.
    
    - com.repmate.safety: SafetyCheckInPreferences (DataStore: toggle + contact),
      CheckInScheduler/CheckInWorkGateway (WorkManager chain), CheckInNotifyWorker/
      CheckInEscalateWorker (HiltWorker), CheckInEscalationAction (the testable
      send-or-skip decision), CheckInAckStore (the "already answered" flag
      CheckInEscalationAction re-checks), LastLocationProvider/SafetyAlertSender
      (FusedLocationProviderClient/SmsManager behind interfaces, each with a fake
      for tests).
    - Profile: disclaimer dialog, SEND_SMS/ACCESS_FINE_LOCATION/POST_NOTIFICATIONS
      request, and an Emergency contact dialog. SEND_SMS denial keeps the feature
      off; the other two degrade gracefully instead.
    - Verified end to end on a real device: notify/escalate timer chain, "I'm OK"
      cancellation, and the real SmsManager send path (fails there only because
      that test device has no SIM installed).
    
    Nothing added to com.repmate.engine.

commit 37c68d01d428fe7af5f411ae223f42eb4aa62f3f
Author:     Mohit Nanda <mohit240903@gmail.com>
AuthorDate: Tue Sep 22 22:12:00 2026 +1000
Commit:     Mohit Nanda <mohit240903@gmail.com>
CommitDate: Tue Sep 22 22:12:00 2026 +1000

    feat(pushup): real camera-driven push-up counting, integrated into Home
    
    Replaces the fake PushupRepDetector (a rep every 1.5s of elapsed frame
    time) with a real elbow-angle state machine, ported from the
    feature/pushup-camera-probe spike now that it's shown a usable signal.
    
    - com.repmate.engine.PushupRepDetector: pure Kotlin, straight/bent
      Schmitt trigger (152.6 / 126.5 degrees, measured), median-of-3
      smoothing. Unit tested (PushupRepDetectorTest), no Android or ML Kit
      types.
    - com.repmate.pose: the pose geometry and arm-locking logic the angle
      stream is built from (Joint/Arm/Tracking, elbowAngleDegrees,
      ArmLock). Also pure Kotlin and unit tested, kept out of
      com.repmate.engine since it's camera/pose-domain rather than the rep
      counter itself.
    - com.repmate.ui.workout.pushup: CameraX + ML Kit wiring
      (PushupPoseAnalyzer), the ViewModel, and the workout screen --
      camera preview, big rep count, tracking status, placement
      instructions, spoken/buzzed reps since the rear-camera screen faces
      away.
    - Home now routes Push-up straight to this screen, skipping the
      calibration gate entirely (no per-user profile exists for push-ups
      yet -- CalibrationUiState.Unsupported is unchanged for any other
      path that still reaches Calibration for this exercise, e.g.
      Profile's Recalibrate picker).
    - LiveWorkoutViewModel's PUSHUP branch is now a degrade-gracefully
      stub: it's no longer reachable via Home, but kept exhaustive against
      a stale deep link rather than removed outright.
    - Reps are saved via SessionRepository like squat and jumping jack, so
      a finished set shows up on Home. Not scored against a calibration
      profile -- there isn't one -- so each rep carries a neutral score
      and an explicit "not scored" reason instead of inventing one.
    
    CameraX + ML Kit pose-detection added as real dependencies (not
    debug-only); CAMERA permission requested at runtime with a rationale
    screen, declared with uses-feature required=false so an install on a
    cameraless device is unaffected.
    
    Not merged -- new branch off main, for a team demo.

commit da3a51b24df697ce801688f9b56eb51ed611ebc8
Author:     Mohit Nanda <mohit240903@gmail.com>
AuthorDate: Sun Sep 20 14:39:42 2026 +1000
Commit:     Mohit Nanda <mohit240903@gmail.com>
CommitDate: Sun Sep 20 14:39:42 2026 +1000

    feat(probe): lock the arm for the set instead of re-picking it every frame
    
    The first real run switched arms 70 times in 60 s, and the angle steps
    by a median of 8 degrees at each switch against 2.4 elsewhere. ArmLock
    picks the nearer arm once, by median depth over the first 15 frames with
    a pose, and never changes it until reset. If the locked arm is missing
    the answer is nothing, not the other arm.
    
    Nothing is counted until the arm has locked. Reset, a camera change and
    a change of counting source re-pick. The screen shows (locked) or
    (choosing), and the log carries locked= and an "arm LOCKED" line with
    the depths it decided on.
    
    Replayed on the first real run, the rule locks the right arm and counts
    12 reps with the new thresholds, against 13 for per-frame switching.
    Seven mutants of the lock are caught by its tests.

commit 4d28f1252dc6ab61e4385921d12d1da12de2ac5c
Author:     Mohit Nanda <mohit240903@gmail.com>
AuthorDate: Sun Sep 20 09:52:49 2026 +1000
Commit:     Mohit Nanda <mohit240903@gmail.com>
CommitDate: Sun Sep 20 09:53:49 2026 +1000

    fix(probe): move the push-up thresholds to 152.6 and 126.5, measured from the first real run
    
    The first guess of 155 / 105 counted 3 of about 11 reps: over 11 clear
    reps the measured tops were 153-178 (median 170) and the bottoms 55-121
    (median 112), so the bent threshold was the blocker, not the straight one.
    The thresholds are now 70% and 25% of the way from the median bottom to
    the median top. One person, one session, front camera: provisional, and
    documented as such. A regression test replays the 11 real reps.

commit bd91aebf7b228f7354f14d6635e2fce9362a9d3b
Author:     Mohit Nanda <mohit240903@gmail.com>
AuthorDate: Sun Sep 20 09:30:34 2026 +1000
Commit:     Mohit Nanda <mohit240903@gmail.com>
CommitDate: Sun Sep 20 09:30:34 2026 +1000

    feat(probe): rep counter, rear camera, and stable tracking for the push-up probe
    
    - PushUpRepCounter: straight -> bent -> straight is one rep, with a
      105/155 degree hysteresis gap and a median-of-3 filter that removes a
      single bad frame. Pure Kotlin. Thresholds are starting values.
    - The count is shown large on screen, with the phase and the tracking
      state; each rep is also spoken and buzzed, since the rear camera leaves
      the screen facing away from the user. Buttons: reset, count from 2D or
      3D, flip camera.
    - Rear camera by default.
    - Per-frame log line moved from DEBUG to INFO (a `PushUpProbe:I` filter
      was hiding every angle) and now carries the in-frame likelihood of all
      six arm landmarks and the number of landmarks ML Kit returned.
    - Arm choice and the tracking label flipped several times a second when
      an arm's weakest landmark hovered around the 0.5 gate, because the gate
      was used as a selector. Both now have hysteresis (enter at 0.5, leave
      below 0.3). Tests reproduce the flip on the old logic.

commit d90faddc8b519214886cf8247fa09bdce90b8433
Author:     Mohit Nanda <mohit240903@gmail.com>
AuthorDate: Mon Sep 14 19:35:53 2026 +1000
Commit:     Mohit Nanda <mohit240903@gmail.com>
CommitDate: Sun Sep 20 09:10:06 2026 +1000

    chore(app): camera push-up probe to test pose detection (not for merge)
    
    A temporary, hand-run probe answering one question before any push-up counting
    is designed: does on-device pose detection give an elbow angle that is stable and
    usable in a push-up position, with the phone propped on the floor beside the user?
    
    PushUpProbeActivity shows the camera preview full screen, runs ML Kit pose
    detection (the base on-device model, bundled, no network or API key) on a CameraX
    analysis stream, and displays the shoulder-elbow-wrist angle large enough to read
    from the floor, for whichever arm is more confidently detected. A tracking
    indicator shows NO PERSON / LOW CONFIDENCE / TRACKING, a one-second min-max spread
    shows whether a held angle is steady, and every analysed frame is logged under the
    PushUpProbe tag. There is no rep counting.
    
    OUT OF v1 SCOPE, and deliberately kept on this branch. CLAUDE.md lists camera and
    pose estimation under the v2 guardrails (no CameraX, no ML Kit, no MediaPipe),
    specifies the app as accelerometer and gyroscope only, and says a single phone
    cannot see joint geometry. This probe tests that premise without changing the
    scope; adopting camera push-ups would be a separate team decision with CLAUDE.md
    updated first. Nothing in com.repmate.engine is touched.
    
    The activity is not a launcher, so the app icon is unchanged. Start it with
      adb shell am start -n com.example.repmate/.PushUpProbeActivity
    
    The angle maths and arm choice live in PoseAngles.kt with no ML Kit or Android
    types, so PoseAnglesTest checks them on the JVM: known angles, the 0 and 180 ends,
    wrap-around, mirror and endpoint symmetry, coincident landmarks, choosing the arm
    by its weakest point, the tracking threshold, and the stability window.
    
    Dependencies: CameraX 1.6.2, the latest stable, and ML Kit pose-detection
    18.0.0-beta5, since Google publishes the current model only as a beta. Cost: the
    debug APK grows to about 109 MB, almost all of it ML Kit's native library bundled
    for four CPU architectures (about 77 MB).
    
    Checked: assembleDebug and testDebugUnitTest pass, 52 unit tests. Not yet run on a
    device.
    
    Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5 <noreply@anthropic.com>
    Claude-Session: https://claude.ai/code/session_015Hqmc34aFKcxsNwVRqw5Gv

commit ddd1ba2514bc67f935c662692ff53fa88b8691cd
Author:     Mohit Nanda <mohit240903@gmail.com>
AuthorDate: Sun Sep 20 08:00:33 2026 +1000
Commit:     Mohit Nanda <mohit240903@gmail.com>
CommitDate: Sun Sep 20 08:00:33 2026 +1000

    fix(leaderboard): a failed leaderboard query must not crash the app
    
    FirestoreLeaderboardRepository closes its flow with the Firestore error, so
    a PERMISSION_DENIED reached HomeViewModel and LeaderboardViewModel as an
    uncaught exception inside viewModelScope.launch and killed the process on
    Home, right after login. The rules themselves are a Firebase console
    matter and are not touched here.
    
    - observeTop(): topPlayers() with its failure turned into a final
      LeaderboardLoad.Failed value. Both ViewModels read the leaderboard
      through it, so a failure is state they render, not an exception.
    - Home shows "Leaderboard unavailable right now" in the leaderboard card;
      the Leaderboard screen shows an unavailable message in place of the list.
      Nothing else on either screen depends on it.
    - The repository logs the Firestore error (tag RepMateLeaderboard) where
      its code is still visible; both ViewModels log again on the failed path.

commit e5452dfa2f9ee014e6431f3be92ca9c056a4a1ea
Author:     Mohit Nanda <mohit240903@gmail.com>
AuthorDate: Sun Sep 20 07:49:40 2026 +1000
Commit:     Mohit Nanda <mohit240903@gmail.com>
CommitDate: Sun Sep 20 07:49:40 2026 +1000

    feat(engine): report every rejected rep window and the guard that dropped it
    
    SquatRepDetector.onRejectedWindow is called whenever a movement window is
    opened and then discarded, with every guard it failed (too short, too
    small, too soon after the last rep, abandoned too long) and the measured
    value against the limit. Purely observational: attaching a listener does
    not change what is counted, which is pinned on all four squat traces.
    
    Calibration logs each rejected window under RepMateCalibration; Live
    Workout logs them under RepMateWorkout plus a per-guard summary when the
    workout ends. A missed rep can now be traced to the threshold that dropped
    it, and by how much, instead of being a silent gap.
    
    Windows that never cross the trigger threshold open no window and so are
    not reported; that is a property of the detector, noted in the log docs.
    
    Co-Authored-By: Claude Sonnet 5 <noreply@anthropic.com>

    feat(calibration): capture calibration from real detected reps
    
    CalibrationViewModel no longer invents its samples. The 1.2 s clock loop
    and buildFakeSamples() are gone; calibration now streams live frames from
    SensorSource into the real detector and passes the five reps it actually
    counts to CalibrationProfile.evaluate(). Only an accepted profile is saved.

    0.94, so calibrating made live squat detection stricter on fabricated data.

      duration floor lowered to 280 ms, so calibration can learn a pace the
      550 ms default drops. Amplitude and cooldown stay at their defaults:
      lowering them admits Hit's 0.83 wobble and Lisa's post-rep rebounds,
      measured and pinned in CalibrationCaptureTest.
    - CalibrationCapture: plain-Kotlin capture session, replayed against all
      six squat and jumping-jack traces in tests.
    - Screen: instructions to rep exactly as in a real set, a 5 s pocket
      countdown, a live rep-by-rep progress row and movement indicator, a
      no-rep hint, and Rejected -> Try again with nothing saved. The
      jumping-jack metronome runs through countdown and capture.
    - Push-ups can't be calibrated (the only detector fabricates reps); the
      screen says so and offers Back.
    - "Continue without calibrating" proceeds on defaults and saves nothing.
    - CalibrationProfile KDoc: permissive capture guards supersede the old
      prompted-window note, with the reasoning.

commit da03ad14e1cf80609bd4f16b371777a9ae168fb5
Author:     Mohit Nanda <mohit240903@gmail.com>
AuthorDate: Thu Sep 17 20:15:19 2026 +1000
Commit:     Mohit Nanda <mohit240903@gmail.com>
CommitDate: Thu Sep 17 20:15:19 2026 +1000

    feat(engine): retain the loudest calibration sample on the profile
    
    evaluate() already measures amplitudes.max() for the MAX_AMPLITUDE_SPREAD
    check but discarded it afterwards, so the spread a set was accepted on could
    not be read back off the profile: a rejected set reports its spread in the
    rejection message, an accepted one reported nothing. Keeping it closes that
    asymmetry and gives softestSampleAmplitude its other end, for the same reason
    the other raw measurements are kept -- so a profile can be explained rather
    than merely applied.
    
    Additive: loudestSampleAmplitude defaults to softestSampleAmplitude, so every
    existing construction still compiles. The default is deliberately not 0f --
    that would break the loudest >= softest invariant any consumer of the pair
    relies on, and would claim an amplitude evaluate() rejects as impossible. A
    profile built without it instead reads as a set with no measured spread.
    
    Both existing call sites use named arguments, so the mid-list position is safe.
    describe() now prints the amplitude band, mirroring how it already prints the
    duration band.
    
    53 -> 54 tests, all passing.
    
    Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5 <noreply@anthropic.com>

 .../com/repmate/engine/CalibrationProfileTest.kt   | 26 ++++++++++++++++++++++

commit 740ae14aa228a0b4d4fff93408f71e5484e6c73d
Author:     Mohit Nanda <mohit240903@gmail.com>
AuthorDate: Thu Sep 17 20:14:55 2026 +1000
Commit:     Mohit Nanda <mohit240903@gmail.com>
CommitDate: Thu Sep 17 20:14:55 2026 +1000

    fix(app): declare SensorProbeActivity in a debug-only manifest
    
    SensorProbeActivity is the engine team's sensor bring-up harness and it is
    android:exported="true", so declaring it in the main manifest shipped it in
    release builds as a component any app on the device could start.
    
    Move the declaration to app/src/debug/AndroidManifest.xml, which the manifest
    merger folds into the debug variant only. This keeps the documented
    `adb shell am start -n com.example.repmate/.SensorProbeActivity` workflow
    working on debug builds while removing the activity from release entirely.
    
    The class itself stays in the main source set because HomeScreen.kt references
    it directly from the BuildConfig.DEBUG-gated button; only the declaration moves.
    
    Verified against both merged manifests: present in debug, absent in release.
    
    Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5 <noreply@anthropic.com>

commit f470d18d5a30d5780c5f9989d3c3e08e3e135c22
Author:     Xue Li (Claire) <xli3326@student.unimelb.edu.au>
AuthorDate: Thu Sep 17 02:03:17 2026 +1000
Commit:     Xue Li (Claire) <xli3326@student.unimelb.edu.au>
CommitDate: Thu Sep 17 02:21:05 2026 +1000

    feat(engine): add SessionReplayer for per-rep motion curve replay
    
    Replays a saved WorkoutSession's frames back through SquatRepDetector
    frame-by-frame, capturing the smoothed magnitude per frame so each
    completed rep gets its own motion curve (sliced by that rep's own
    startMs..endMs window) alongside its FormScorer score. No changes to
    RepDetector.kt needed -- this reuses the existing process()/
    smoothedMagnitude API instead of the processAll() convenience method.
    
    Returns null when WorkoutSession.frames is absent (a real storage
    implementation may choose not to persist it), matching the existing
    degrade-gracefully pattern used by RepScore.pauseSeconds and
    rangePercent.

commit e09107aa302bfc20023c2e5c0dbc9aa532460d44
Author:     Xue Li (Claire) <xli3326@student.unimelb.edu.au>
AuthorDate: Wed Sep 16 20:28:55 2026 +1000
Commit:     Xue Li (Claire) <xli3326@student.unimelb.edu.au>
CommitDate: Wed Sep 16 20:28:55 2026 +1000

    fix(engine): make consistency check one-sided against calibration reference
    
    Reps deeper/more forceful than the calibration reference were being
    penalized the same as reps that were shallower, causing false positives
    on genuinely correct sets whose amplitude climbs while settling into the
    movement (e.g. squat_10_pocket). Only shortfall below the reference now
    counts toward the deviation, matching how depth scoring already treats
    depthRatio >= 1 as unconditionally good. Threshold value (0.35f) unchanged
    -- verified squat_10_pocket now scores 0.000 deviation on every rep.

commit 3c8cb6589e70f1c854510cb1446cc4fec1a17697
Author:     Xue Li (Claire) <xli3326@student.unimelb.edu.au>
AuthorDate: Tue Sep 15 13:26:01 2026 +1000
Commit:     Xue Li (Claire) <xli3326@student.unimelb.edu.au>
CommitDate: Tue Sep 15 13:49:31 2026 +1000

    fix(engine): stop scoring depth and consistency for uncalibrated users
    
    Depth and consistency scoring used a fixed placeholder amplitude
    (FALLBACK_REFERENCE_AMPLITUDE = 1.4f) for users with no CalibrationProfile.
    Mohit re-measured all 43 recorded reps across 4 participants and found
    genuine squat amplitude spans roughly 1.03-9.05 (about 9x) -- no single
    placeholder is fair to all of them. A fixed low value makes a strong
    squatter look permanently "too deep"; a fixed high value makes a light
    squatter look permanently "not deep enough" and could push someone to
    change an already-correct squat to chase a misleading score.
    
    Uncalibrated users now get an honest "not scored" sentinel instead:
    rangePercent = NOT_MEASURABLE_RANGE_PERCENT (-1), and reasons carries
    "not calibrated: depth not scored" instead of "good depth"/"not deep
    enough". This mirrors the existing pauseSeconds = 0f precedent. The
    consistency check is gated the same way, since it anchors to the same
    softestSampleAmplitude reference depth scoring uses and has no reliable
    reference for an uncalibrated user either.
    
    Tempo scoring is unaffected in structure, but the uncalibrated fallback
    window is tightened from 600-2000ms to 600-1800ms per the same 43-rep
    data (pooled real reps ran 615-3153ms, with 3153ms a likely outlier).
    FALLBACK_REFERENCE_AMPLITUDE is removed entirely.
    
    Discussed with Mohit in team chat; he suggested this approach after
    the amplitude re-measurement.
    
    All 52 unit tests pass (11 in FormScorerTest, replacing the old
    "works without a calibration profile using the fallback reference"
    test with three tests covering the sentinel range, the skipped
    consistency check, and the tightened tempo window).

 app/src/main/java/com/repmate/engine/FormScorer.kt | 131 ++++++++++++++-------

commit 0787724e437360b62c8f9343bb7e9e71b65008b6
Author:     Mohit Nanda <mohit240903@gmail.com>
AuthorDate: Mon Sep 14 19:35:53 2026 +1000
Commit:     Mohit Nanda <mohit240903@gmail.com>
CommitDate: Mon Sep 14 19:35:53 2026 +1000

    chore(app): camera push-up probe to test pose detection (not for merge)
    
    A temporary, hand-run probe answering one question before any push-up counting
    is designed: does on-device pose detection give an elbow angle that is stable and
    usable in a push-up position, with the phone propped on the floor beside the user?
    
    PushUpProbeActivity shows the camera preview full screen, runs ML Kit pose
    detection (the base on-device model, bundled, no network or API key) on a CameraX
    analysis stream, and displays the shoulder-elbow-wrist angle large enough to read
    from the floor, for whichever arm is more confidently detected. A tracking
    indicator shows NO PERSON / LOW CONFIDENCE / TRACKING, a one-second min-max spread
    shows whether a held angle is steady, and every analysed frame is logged under the
    PushUpProbe tag. There is no rep counting.
    
    OUT OF v1 SCOPE, and deliberately kept on this branch. CLAUDE.md lists camera and
    pose estimation under the v2 guardrails (no CameraX, no ML Kit, no MediaPipe),
    specifies the app as accelerometer and gyroscope only, and says a single phone
    cannot see joint geometry. This probe tests that premise without changing the
    scope; adopting camera push-ups would be a separate team decision with CLAUDE.md
    updated first. Nothing in com.repmate.engine is touched.
    
    The activity is not a launcher, so the app icon is unchanged. Start it with
      adb shell am start -n com.example.repmate/.PushUpProbeActivity
    
    The angle maths and arm choice live in PoseAngles.kt with no ML Kit or Android
    types, so PoseAnglesTest checks them on the JVM: known angles, the 0 and 180 ends,
    wrap-around, mirror and endpoint symmetry, coincident landmarks, choosing the arm
    by its weakest point, the tracking threshold, and the stability window.
    
    Dependencies: CameraX 1.6.2, the latest stable, and ML Kit pose-detection
    18.0.0-beta5, since Google publishes the current model only as a beta. Cost: the
    debug APK grows to about 109 MB, almost all of it ML Kit's native library bundled
    for four CPU architectures (about 77 MB).
    
    Checked: assembleDebug and testDebugUnitTest pass, 52 unit tests. Not yet run on a
    device.
    
    Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5 <noreply@anthropic.com>
    Claude-Session: https://claude.ai/code/session_015Hqmc34aFKcxsNwVRqw5Gv

commit 92e5c384877e6a154b61bc9c8346d421769142ad

    test(engine): cover CalibrationProfile
    
    Nothing tested calibration: MIN_DURATION_MARGIN went 0.85 -> 0.75 and the whole
    suite stayed green. Twelve tests now cover the margins, both duration bounds, the
    amplitude, max-duration and cooldown bounds at each end, the consistency gate
    (amplitude and duration spread each at its boundary, too few samples, overlapping
    reps), and the fallback to the tuned defaults when calibration is rejected.
    
    Every expected value is a literal. A test that recomputed shortest *
    MIN_DURATION_MARGIN would follow the constant wherever it went, sharing the blind
    spot this file exists to close.
    
    The fallback is checked through behaviour, since the detector's thresholds are
    private: a detector built from a rejected calibration must report exactly the
    default detector's events on every recorded squat trace. A second test shows that
    comparison can tell the difference -- an accepted profile changes what
    squat_10_hit reports -- so the fallback test cannot pass vacuously.
    
    Mutation-checked; each of these breaks at least one test: margin 0.75 -> 0.85,
    duration floor 280 -> 400, duration ceiling 1200 -> 1300, the duration clamp
    removed, amplitude margin 0.65 -> 0.70, amplitude floor 0.35 -> 0.30, cooldown
    ceiling 900 -> 1000, amplitude spread limit 3.0 -> 2.5 and > to >=, duration
    spread limit 2.0 -> 2.5, required samples 5 -> 4, and the fallback cooldown
    default -> 2000 ms.
    
    Checked on this tree: assembleDebug and testDebugUnitTest pass, 41 unit tests.

commit 5d2326c26a87ebdf5de6ab54ef1ce556e5a85943
Author:     Mohit Nanda <mohit240903@gmail.com>
AuthorDate: Mon Sep 14 19:15:55 2026 +1000
Commit:     Mohit Nanda <mohit240903@gmail.com>
CommitDate: Mon Sep 14 19:15:55 2026 +1000

    fix(engine): lower the calibrated duration floor to 280 ms
    
    MIN_REP_DURATION_FLOOR_MS 400 -> 280.
    
    Genuine live reps measured 300 ms and 320 ms. A 400 ms floor rejected them
    whatever calibration derived, because it clamps any lower derived value straight
    back up: no margin, and no amount of calibrating at a natural pace, could have
    recovered them. 280 sits 20 ms under the shortest genuine rep measured.
    
    It removes a block rather than guaranteeing a count. A 300 ms rep is counted only
    if the shortest calibration rep is 400 ms or less (400 x 0.75 = 300); calibrate at
    a slower pace and the derived floor rejects it again. That is the guidance half of
    the calibration-vs-performance problem recorded in 7997d2a.
    
    Checked on the four recorded squat traces. None derives a floor near 280 (482-674
    ms), so through calibration nothing changes on them. Replayed with a detector
    running at 280 ms directly, every set window still counts 10 of 10 and both quiet
    windows stay empty. Over whole recordings it admits phone-handling bursts outside
    the set: with default guards, one on squat_10_fast (282 ms) and one on
    squat_10_lisa (312 ms); with calibrated guards, two on squat_10_hit before its set
    starts. The KDoc records these, replacing the note that kept the floor at 400.
    
    Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5 <noreply@anthropic.com>
    Claude-Session: https://claude.ai/code/session_015Hqmc34aFKcxsNwVRqw5Gv

commit 7997d2a5e7e9bb770c3bbd3e70242cb362d2a1ff
Author:     Mohit Nanda <mohit240903@gmail.com>
AuthorDate: Mon Sep 14 19:00:06 2026 +1000
Commit:     Mohit Nanda <mohit240903@gmail.com>
CommitDate: Mon Sep 14 19:00:06 2026 +1000

    fix(engine): derive the calibrated duration floor 25% below the shortest rep
    
    MIN_DURATION_MARGIN 0.85 -> 0.75: the derived minRepDurationMs now sits a quarter
    below the shortest calibration rep instead of 15% below it.
    
    A calibration set is a sample, not a bound. The shortest of five prompted reps is
    not the shortest rep the user will produce, and people squat faster in a real set
    than in a paced five-rep calibration, so a floor fitted tightly to the calibration
    minimum rejects genuine reps from the set that follows.
    
    Checked against all four recorded squat traces: profile derived from reps 1-5,
    then replayed over the set window, the whole recording and the quiet window, at
    0.70, 0.75, 0.80 and 0.85. All four margins behave identically -- 10 of 10 in
    every set, nothing in either quiet window, no window admitted or lost relative to
    0.85. On the data that exists the change admits nothing. Derived floors move from
    546-764 ms to 482-674 ms.
    
    What it gives up, now recorded in the KDoc, is redundancy on Hit's 743 ms wobble.
    The old note claimed duration was the only guard that could reject it under
    calibration. It never was: his clamped 900 ms cooldown rejects it too, because it
    starts 778 ms after the preceding rep. At 0.85 both guards caught it; at 0.75 only
    the cooldown does, by 122 ms, and with the cooldown disabled Hit's set reads 11.
    
    Also recorded, because it is the honest result: in live testing the margin
    recovered only 1 of 4 missed reps. The rest is a calibration-vs-performance
    mismatch rather than a threshold problem. The user's paced calibration reps
    averaged 7.0 amplitude; the set counted minutes later averaged 1.3. Every derived
    threshold is fitted to the first population and applied to the second, and that
    has to be solved by guidance -- calibrate at the pace and depth you train at. The
    margin and the guidance are complementary halves of the fix.
    
    MIN_REP_DURATION_FLOOR_MS stays at 400. Its KDoc no longer claims that no squat
    completes in under 400 ms, which live reps of 380-420 ms contradict, and now says
    why it is kept rather than lowered.
    
    Live figures come from probe logs, not committed fixtures. Nothing in the suite
    tests CalibrationProfile directly, so this margin is not pinned by a test.
    
    Checked on exactly this tree: assembleDebug and testDebugUnitTest pass.

Author:     Mohit Nanda <mohit240903@gmail.com>

    chore(app): live test rig for both detectors, on its own branch
    
    A temporary, hand-run harness for exercising the squat and jumping-jack detectors
    live on a phone. Until a real workout screen exists, it is the only way to run the
    detectors against DeviceSensorSource rather than recorded traces.
    
    THIS IS A TEMPORARY TEST RIG, not app code: no ViewModel, the detector is driven
    straight from the collector, and UI state lives on the activity. It is kept on
    this branch and is not meant to merge into main.
    
    Over the older probe already on main it adds:
    - a toggle between SquatRepDetector and JumpingJackRepDetector, resetting the
      count on every switch;
    - a drift-free metronome for paced jumping-jack sets, defaulting to 52 BPM, the
      pooled cadence of the two recordings those thresholds were validated against;
    - paced squat calibration through CalibrationProfile;
    - a WINDOW log line for every window the trigger opens, accepted or not, naming
      the guard that rejected it. The detectors cannot report rejections, so a
      guard-free BurstDetector shadow re-applies the guards in the detector's own
      order, and ProbeShadowTest replays all six traces requiring the shadow's
      verdicts to match the real detectors frame by frame.
    
    SensorProbeActivity remains the launcher on this branch. This version stays off
    main; main still carries the older probe as its launcher until the launcher is
    moved back to MainActivity.
    
    The removal checklist is in SensorProbeActivity's KDoc:
      1. Delete SensorProbeActivity.kt and ProbeShadowTest.kt.
      2. In AndroidManifest.xml, delete the .SensorProbeActivity entry and uncomment
         MainActivity's MAIN/LAUNCHER intent-filter.
    
    Checked on exactly this tree: assembleDebug and testDebugUnitTest pass.

commit c09237c4fbacdd82a7d7ee1890aee5ab26a8312b
Author:     Mohit Nanda <mohit240903@gmail.com>
AuthorDate: Sat Sep 12 20:06:54 2026 +1000
Commit:     Mohit Nanda <mohit240903@gmail.com>
CommitDate: Sat Sep 12 20:08:11 2026 +1000

    feat(data): define the SessionRepository seam
    
    The contract between producing a workout and storing one. The engine's job ends
    with a WorkoutSession; what happens to it next -- Room, Firestore, both -- is not
    the engine's business, and this interface is where that stops being its business.
    
    Interface only, no implementation, deliberately. The point is that the two sides
    are written independently against a fixed contract: a caller can work against a
    ten-line in-memory fake before any table exists, and swap to the real thing
    through Hilt without changing a line. An interface shipped next to its one
    obvious implementation would just be indirection.
    
    Defined here rather than in the data layer because WorkoutSession is an engine
    model and frozen -- the shape of what gets stored is settled before storage is.
    The implementation belongs under com.repmate.data.local (Room) or
    com.repmate.cloud (remote), bound to this interface by a Hilt module. The Hilt
    graph for that already exists on main.
    
    Package placement follows CLAUDE.md, which reserves com.repmate.data.repo for
    repository interfaces. Note the persistence code currently on main sits in
    com.example.repmate.data / .di rather than the com.repmate.* paths CLAUDE.md
    specifies; that is worth reconciling while it is still ~90 lines, but it is not
    this commit's business.
    
    The KDoc pins what callers may assume and leaves the rest open: save() replaces
    by id, so a retry or a resume leaves one row and not two, and must not crash a
    workout the user already performed; recent() is a Flow ordered newest-first that
    emits current contents on collection and again on every change, because the
    natural consumer is a screen that should not need telling to refresh. Storage
    engine, table shape, threading and caching are all the implementation's to
    choose.
    
    One question is flagged rather than decided, because it is a storage question:
    WorkoutSession.frames can hold ~3,000 samples for a 30-second recording and is
    nullable precisely so it can be dropped. Persist it, drop it or store it
    separately -- but knowingly, not via a SELECT * that turns every history read
    into megabytes.
    
    No behaviour change: nothing implements or consumes this yet. 26 tests still
    pass.
    
    Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5 <noreply@anthropic.com>
    Claude-Session: https://claude.ai/code/session_01ADCaD2oMcR1z9hjuwJdkTA

commit 3ddc39d7932874f816208aec7d8a4e0b93ea1030
Author:     Mohit Nanda <mohit240903@gmail.com>
AuthorDate: Sat Sep 12 19:57:20 2026 +1000
Commit:     Mohit Nanda <mohit240903@gmail.com>
CommitDate: Sat Sep 12 19:59:24 2026 +1000

    test(engine): add two paced jumping-jack traces as fixtures
    
    The first recordings in the library for an exercise other than the squat, and the
    evidence JumpingJackRepDetector's thresholds actually rest on. Both are Mohit,
    Pixel, ~99 Hz, front pocket, 10 reps, performed to a metronome; the second at a
    faster setting. TotalAcceleration.csv from each Sensor Logger export, the same
    channel the Android squat traces use, so gravity is present and the thresholds
    have a resting ~9.81 to sit against.
    
    Both count 10 of 10 in the set window on thresholds that were fixed before either
    file existed, with no retuning. Measured across the pair:
    
      surviving bursts per rep    exactly 2.0 on both (20 kept from 26 and 22 raw)
      jump-out amplitude          9.05 - 11.72
      landing amplitude          12.03 - 23.50
      landing harder than its
        own jump-out             20 of 20 pairs
      rebounds rejected           0.50 - 1.87, over 51 - 121 ms
      shortest genuine impact     283 ms
    
    fullTraceEvents is 10 for both -- the same as the in-set count, which is not what
    the squat traces do. Every squat trace reports 12 over the raw file: 10 reps plus
    two phone-handling bursts that clear every guard. Pairing removes them here for
    free, because a lone handling burst never finds a harder partner. Requiring two
    ordered impacts rejects single-burst artifacts in a way the one-burst-per-rep
    squat model cannot.
    
    Only the acceleration channel is committed. The raw exports also contain
    Location.csv and a microphone recording, neither of which is here and neither of
    which any part of rep detection needs.
    
    What these two do NOT establish is generality: same participant, same phone,
    recorded minutes apart. They fix the model and the shape of the signal, not the
    spread of it across people. A second participant is the next thing this library
    needs.

commit 82c36900d86e130d99e19cb4404b99fe546a7971
Author:     Mohit Nanda <mohit240903@gmail.com>
AuthorDate: Sat Sep 12 19:56:53 2026 +1000
Commit:     Mohit Nanda <mohit240903@gmail.com>
CommitDate: Sat Sep 12 19:59:24 2026 +1000

    fix(engine): run the detector each trace's exercise actually names
    
    The .expect format has carried an `exercise` key since the first recording, and
    the parser validates it against ExerciseType and rejects anything unknown. Every
    trace test then ignored it and hardcoded SquatRepDetector.
    
    That was harmless while every recording was a squat. It stops being harmless the
    moment a jumping-jack trace is added: the suite would replay it through the squat
    detector, and the resulting miscount would read as a threshold problem rather
    than as a wiring one -- the most expensive kind of wrong signal for a fixture to
    send, because the obvious response is to retune a detector that was never the one
    running.
    
    Adds LabelledTrace.detect(frames), which dispatches on expectation.exercise, and
    points the four trace-iterating tests at it. The choice sits beside setWindow()
    for the same reason that does: this class is where "what this recording means" is
    decided once for the whole suite, rather than re-decided in each test.
    
    PUSHUP throws rather than falling back to a squat detector. A silent fallback
    would report a plausible-looking number for an exercise nothing has been written
    for, which is exactly the failure the .expect format exists to prevent.
    
    The accompanying test replays one set of frames under both labels and requires
    the answers to differ, so this cannot regress into being ignored again without
    something going red.
    
    No behaviour change for the squat traces: they resolve to the same
    SquatRepDetector they were already getting, and their counts are unchanged
    (10/10/10/10 in-set; 12/12/11/12 over the whole file).

commit 99fdd5b153126a3888c86d1d7b521a80d41da172
Author:     Mohit Nanda <mohit240903@gmail.com>
AuthorDate: Sat Sep 12 19:56:15 2026 +1000
Commit:     Mohit Nanda <mohit240903@gmail.com>
CommitDate: Sat Sep 12 19:59:24 2026 +1000

    feat(engine): count jumping jacks by pairing impacts
    
    A jumping jack is two impacts, not one movement. The jump out and the landing
    each open and close the Schmitt trigger, so SquatRepDetector's one-burst-per-rep
    model reads a clean 10-rep set as 20. JumpingJackRepDetector discards rebounds,
    then pairs consecutive surviving impacts soft-then-hard into one RepEvent.
    
    BurstDetector is the shared layer. It does not reimplement the magnitude, the
    250 ms time-based filter or the 10.15/9.7 trigger: it holds a SquatRepDetector
    with the three rep-level guards opened all the way up (minRepDurationMs 1,
    minAmplitude 0, cooldownMs 0), so what comes back is the raw trigger output.
    Nothing on the squat path is modified or duplicated -- RepDetector.kt is
    byte-identical. That is composition rather than an extracted base class on
    purpose: lifting the filter out would mean editing code pinned by replay tests
    against four participants, which is not worth doing for a second exercise. If the
    squat path is ever opened for refactoring, the dependency should be inverted and
    SquatRepDetector should delegate to BurstDetector. maxRepDurationMs is
    deliberately left on, so the never-closing-window protection is inherited.
    
    Thresholds were derived BEFORE any jumping-jack recording was in the suite, from
    reported burst statistics, and then replayed against the fixtures WITHOUT being
    retuned:
    
      minBurstAmplitude   4.1   geometric centre of the rebound/impact gap.
                                Predicted from 1.9 and 9.0; measured 1.87 and 9.05,
                                whose geometric centre is 4.11. Sits 2.19x above the
                                largest rebound and 2.21x below the softest impact.
      minBurstDurationMs  130   9 ms above the longest rebound (121 ms), 153 ms below
                                the shortest genuine impact (283 ms).
    
    Geometric rather than arithmetic because impact and rebound scale together with
    effort, so the margin that matters is multiplicative.
    
    The duration guard is redundant on the data that exists: across both recordings
    it rejects nothing the amplitude floor has not already rejected. It is kept as a
    cheap second opinion because the two fail in different directions, and because
    amplitude is the guard carrying a single participant's evidence. The clearest
    case for that ordering is the 0.66-amplitude, 877 ms drift opening the fast
    trace: duration would have kept it, amplitude threw it out.
    
    Pairing tests only that the landing is larger than its own jump-out, not an
    absolute soft/hard boundary. An absolute 12.0 would have worked on both fixtures
    -- but on the slower one the nearest landing clears it by 0.03 m/s^2, which is a
    coincidence rather than a margin. The relative test is physics rather than one
    person's mass and held for 20 of 20 pairs. The observed boundary is kept as a
    constant for a future calibration step.
    
    Also documented as a product requirement rather than an implementation detail:
    the pairing model needs a PACED set. Unpaced, the ratio collapses to 1.2
    bursts/rep at a ~1 s cadence, because a gentle take-off never lifts the smoothed
    magnitude clear of the trigger. No pairing rule recovers an impact that is not in
    the signal, and lowering the trigger far enough to catch it would admit the
    rebounds two guards exist to reject.
    
    Limits of the evidence: two paced recordings, one participant, one phone, taken
    minutes apart. Amplitude is the most person-dependent quantity in the detector
    and the squat path already shows what a floor set by one person costs. A SECOND
    PARTICIPANT is the obvious next step; the figure to watch is the softest
    jump-out, since that is what the 4.1 floor sits under.
    
    Tests are split by what they are good for: the replay traces establish whether
    the numbers are right, and the synthetic signal here establishes whether the
    logic is right -- resynchronising from a mid-rep start and holding an unpaired
    jump-out cannot be provoked by either recording. Every guard and both pairing
    rules are mutation-checked.
    
    Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5 <noreply@anthropic.com>
    Claude-Session: https://claude.ai/code/session_01ADCaD2oMcR1z9hjuwJdkTA

Author:     Mohit Nanda <mohit240903@gmail.com>

    chore(traces): keep the raw Sensor Logger exports alongside the fixtures
    
    The committed traces are one channel each, lifted out of a full Sensor Logger
    export. The other channels are the only copy of data two open detector problems
    need: Gyroscope.csv (all four participants) and Gravity.csv (two of four) are
    the way to recover movement direction, which is what would restore a second
    guard independent of minAmplitude. Re-recording means getting four people back
    together, so the exports are kept here rather than on one laptop.
    
    Two things are removed before first commit, because this repository is public.
    Location.csv is stripped from the pocket and fast zips, which carried 50 and 36
    precise GPS fixes of where they were recorded. And the device id in Metadata.csv
    -- a per-install UUID, stable across every recording from that phone -- is
    blanked in all four, since for Hit and Lisa it identifies a device belonging to
    someone outside the team.
    
    Those are the only alterations. Every other entry in every zip is byte-identical
    to the original export, verified entry by entry: Metadata.csv is the sole
    differing entry in each archive, and device id the sole changed field within it.
    The channel each committed trace was lifted from is untouched, so the fixtures
    remain exactly derivable from these files. The unstripped exports stay off-repo.
    Hit's and Lisa's exports never contained a location channel.
    
    The README records which channel produced which trace, what each zip actually
    contains, and a two-step cleaning checklist for future exports, since removing
    either item after a push means rewriting shared history.
    
    Nothing builds or tests against these files; the suite still reads only
    app/src/test/resources/traces/.

commit 51cd409f51948612061936ee58ce59f09885bb05
Author:     Mohit Nanda <mohit240903@gmail.com>
AuthorDate: Thu Sep 10 22:04:07 2026 +1000
Commit:     Mohit Nanda <mohit240903@gmail.com>
CommitDate: Thu Sep 10 22:04:07 2026 +1000

    feat(engine): let SquatRepDetector run on a CalibrationProfile
    
    Adds a secondary constructor taking a nullable CalibrationProfile. A user who has
    calibrated gets their own minAmplitude, minRepDurationMs, maxRepDurationMs and
    cooldownMs; null falls back to the tuned defaults, so an uncalibrated user — or
    one whose calibration was rejected — keeps working exactly as before.
    
    The seven default literals move into named constants on the companion so the
    fallback can reference them instead of repeating the numbers, which is what would
    let the two copies drift apart. Every value is unchanged: 10.15, 9.7, 550, 3000,
    500, 0.94, 250.
    
    Only the four thresholds calibration can measure are taken from the profile.
    highThreshold and lowThreshold stay fixed: they are positions relative to gravity,
    which is the same on every phone and for every person, so there is nothing
    personal in them to derive. smoothingWindowMs stays fixed too — it is a property
    of the signal rather than the user, it is already rate-invariant, and changing it
    per user would move every amplitude the profile had just been measured in.
    
    Validated by replaying all four traces: derive a profile from reps 1-5, then
    detect reps 6-10 with it. Calibrated scores 5/5 on every trace, matching the fixed
    defaults exactly. That is the expected result and not a disappointing one — the
    defaults were tuned on these same traces, so this comparison is in-sample and the
    defaults cannot lose it. What it establishes is that calibration is not a
    regression, and that the derived thresholds are wide enough to survive the drift
    between a set's two halves. The derived floors span 0.67 to 5.18 against the
    single fixed 0.94, which is the coupling this removes.
    
    Known limitation, unaddressed here: calibrating these four guards does not make a
    weak user detectable. Replaying the traces with movement scaled down shows the
    Schmitt trigger failing first — Hit's weakest rep at half force peaks at 10.26
    against a 10.15 highThreshold, so its window barely opens or does not open at all,
    and no post-hoc guard can recover a rep the state machine never saw. Fixing that
    needs a trigger expressed relative to the user's own peak deviation rather than as
    a fixed offset from gravity, which is a change to the state machine and not to
    these thresholds.
    
    Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5 <noreply@anthropic.com>
    Claude-Session: https://claude.ai/code/session_01Rbqe9krFaMvd49wweHyxsW

commit 98b95770c213ef72feaa4c65112568c3484eccc3
Author:     Mohit Nanda <mohit240903@gmail.com>
AuthorDate: Thu Sep 10 22:03:36 2026 +1000
Commit:     Mohit Nanda <mohit240903@gmail.com>
CommitDate: Thu Sep 10 22:03:36 2026 +1000

    feat(engine): derive per-user thresholds from a calibration set
    
    Absolute thresholds do not transfer between people. Across the four traces the
    same exercise, done correctly, spans 7.1x in amplitude: Hit's softest rep swings
    1.03, Lisa's 7.31. A single minAmplitude has to sit under 1.03 to keep Hit's
    softest rep and over his 0.83 wobble — a band 0.15 wide — while Lisa's reps sit
    eight times above it and constrain nothing. The floor is therefore set by whoever
    squats softest, and a fifth person softer than Hit would break it.
    
    CalibrationProfile derives minAmplitude, minRepDurationMs, maxRepDurationMs and
    cooldownMs from five sample reps, so each user is measured against themselves.
    
    The margins come from what the traces do, not from round numbers. The evidence is
    the drift between a set's first five reps and its last five, which is exactly the
    extrapolation a calibration set must survive:
    
      trace            amplitude   shortest   longest   fastest gap
      squat_10_fast        0.771      1.379     1.202         1.091
      squat_10_hit         1.102      1.288     1.214         1.137
      squat_10_lisa        0.917      1.187     1.040         0.981
      squat_10_pocket      1.287      0.885     1.041         1.236
    
    - AMPLITUDE_MARGIN 0.65: worst softening seen is 0.771 (Mohit fading 4.42 -> 2.92
      as he tires); the symmetric worst case in log space is 0.777. 0.65 sits 16%
      below. Fatigue makes later reps softer, so this margin gets exercised most.
    - MIN_DURATION_MARGIN 0.85: bounded both sides and the band is narrow. At most
      0.885 or Mohit's normal set loses its 615 ms rep; more than 0.826 or Hit's
      743 ms wobble clears a threshold derived from his 899 ms minimum. For Hit this
      guard is the only one that can reject that wobble, since his calibrated
      amplitude floor lands at 0.67, below the wobble's 0.83.
    - MAX_DURATION_MARGIN 2.2: must exceed 1.214 (Hit lengthening 1296 -> 1573 ms)
      and stay under 4.03 or Lisa's 3153 ms settling burst is admitted. 2.2 is the
      geometric centre.
    - COOLDOWN_MARGIN 0.75: only bounded above; worst contraction 0.981, symmetric
      worst 0.809, so 0.75 with 7% to spare.
    
    Two independent safety mechanisms, failing in opposite directions. Rejection
    (evaluate) refuses a set whose five samples disagree with each other: more than 3x
    in amplitude or 2x in duration. Clamping bounds each derived value to a plausible
    range, catching sets that are self-consistent but wrong — five identical bounces,
    five slow heaves — where rejection has nothing to fire on. Bounds are physical on
    one side and measured on the other: minAmplitude 0.35-8.0 (a still phone swings
    0.022; Lisa, the most forceful recorded, derives 5.18), minRepDurationMs 400-1200,
    maxRepDurationMs 1600-3000 (just above the 1573 ms longest genuine rep; the
    ceiling matches the default because Lisa's stuck window is 3153 ms), cooldownMs
    200-900.
    
    FINDING — the cooldown ceiling fires on real data, on two of four traces. Hit
    derives 2086 ms and Mohit's normal set 2207 ms, both clamped to 900 ms. Harmless
    for them: they rest ~3 s between reps, so a shorter cooldown simply stops binding
    and detection is unchanged. It matters because an unclamped 2207 ms cooldown is a
    landmine. That user calibrates at a slow pace, then trains at Mohit's fast pace
    where the tightest genuine gap is 917 ms, and every rep starting inside 2207 ms of
    the last one is discarded as rebound — which is precisely the 2000 ms rep-eating
    bug that 414ba79 removed, reintroduced per-user through the back door. The ceiling
    sits just under that 917 ms so a slow calibration cannot poison a fast workout.
    
    FINDING — the consistency gates do NOT catch wobble-contaminated captures. A
    calibration polluted by Hit's 743 ms / 0.83 wobble spreads 2.24x in amplitude and
    1.58x in duration, inside both the 3x and 2x limits, so it is accepted and pulls
    his floor from 0.67 down to 0.54. The gates are sized against genuine sets, whose
    widest spreads are Hit's own 1.81x and 1.44x, and there is no room to tighten them
    far enough without rejecting real sets. Contamination has to be prevented at
    capture time by measuring only inside a prompted rep window; the gates are a
    backstop against gross nonsense, not a substitute. This is recorded in the KDoc so
    the gate is not mistaken for protection it does not give.
    
    Every derived value and every clamp is recorded in notes and rendered by
    describe(), so a miscounting user's profile is diagnosable from a log without
    reproducing their session. Rejections come back as a typed CalibrationOutcome
    rather than a bare null.
    
    No behaviour change: nothing constructs a profile yet, and the tuned defaults are
    untouched.
    
    Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5 <noreply@anthropic.com>

    chore(app): add the temporary sensor bring-up probe
    
    A throwaway harness that drives SquatRepDetector from live DeviceSensorSource
    frames, so the thresholds tuned on recorded traces can be checked against a phone
    in a pocket. It is explicitly not the app: no ViewModel, detector driven straight
    from the collector, UI state on the activity. It exists to be run by hand, read
    off Logcat (tag RepMateProbe), and deleted.
    
    The manifest moves the launcher to it and comments out MainActivity's
    intent-filter, both marked with the same warning and with removal instructions.

    smoothingWindow was 25 samples, ~250 ms on the ~100 Hz traces but ~500 ms on this
    source's 50 Hz, and the suggested workaround was passing smoothingWindow = 13 by
    hand. That is now fixed upstream and the parameter no longer exists, so the KDoc
    records what actually remains open: whether thresholds tuned on recorded traces
    hold on a live stream, which is a different question. It points at minAmplitude as
    the guard to watch, since it is the only one now separating a wobble from a rep
    and both edges of its band come from one participant.

commit ffd25b50eac00ee767722b73d3a75ffa465d3ceb
Author:     Mohit Nanda <mohit240903@gmail.com>
AuthorDate: Wed Sep 9 01:36:32 2026 +1000
Commit:     Mohit Nanda <mohit240903@gmail.com>
CommitDate: Wed Sep 9 01:36:32 2026 +1000

    fix(engine): abandon rep windows that stay open too long
    
    The Schmitt trigger closes a window when the smoothed signal falls under
    lowThreshold (9.7). A phone lying still reads ~9.81, which sits between
    lowThreshold and highThreshold (10.15), so a burst that ends with the phone parked
    opens a window nothing ever closes. On squat_10_hit that window ran from 100.9 s
    to 137.8 s and was reported as one rep: 36.9 seconds of a still phone counted as a
    squat.
    
    Adds maxRepDurationMs, default 3000 ms. The longest genuine rep across the corpus
    is 1573 ms (Hit's tenth squat), so this leaves 1427 ms of headroom, near twice the
    longest rep, while still clearing the 2195 ms phone-into-pocket burst that Hit's
    fullTraceEvents deliberately counts. It is meant as a statement about human
    movement rather than a tuned number: no single squat runs three seconds from
    movement onset to movement end.
    
    The check sits mid-flight, not at close time. Discarding the window when it finally
    closes would fix the miscount and leave the real defect, because for those 36.9 s
    the detector is stuck in DESCENDING and cannot open a rep at all — a live user
    would be squatting into a counter that had gone deaf. Abandoning returns it to IDLE
    immediately. A window abandoned while the signal is still elevated also sets
    awaitingLowCrossing, so no new window opens until the signal has come back under
    lowThreshold; without it one long burst would produce a train of abandonments every
    3 s, and any of them with enough swing would count.
    
    Counts are unchanged everywhere except the event this exists to remove. All set
    windows still read 10; both quiet windows still yield 0. Hit's whole-recording
    count goes 13 -> 12 and its .expect is updated to match, with the reason recorded
    there. The other twelve events are byte-identical, the 2195 ms burst included.
    
    The new test reduces the failure to its essentials — a burst, a 29.6 s park at
    9.81, then a genuine rep — and asserts both halves: the park yields nothing, and
    the rep after it is still counted, which is what a close-time check would not give.
