commit 8385a0a7e586a015adeb0d2667262ede595ce719
Author:     Mohit Nanda <mohit240903@gmail.com>
AuthorDate: Wed Sep 9 01:35:59 2026 +1000
Commit:     Mohit Nanda <mohit240903@gmail.com>
CommitDate: Wed Sep 9 01:35:59 2026 +1000

    fix(engine): specify the smoothing window in ms, not samples
    
    The moving average was 25 samples, which is 250 ms only at 100 Hz. The same 25
    samples measured 250 ms on the two ~99 Hz Pixel traces, 430 ms on the ~58 Hz
    Samsung trace, and 500 ms on the 50 Hz stream DeviceSensorSource delivers, so the
    filter — and with it every threshold measured on its output — was silently
    re-tuned by whatever rate the device happened to give us.
    
    smoothingWindow: Int = 25 becomes smoothingWindowMs: Long = 250. Each frame evicts
    whatever has aged past the window, so the sample count is derived from the observed
    frame timing rather than fixed: ~25 samples at 99 Hz, ~15 at 58 Hz, ~13 at 50 Hz,
    all covering the same 250 ms. Eviction works on the timestamps directly rather than
    on an estimated rate, so there is no warm-up before the rate is known and a rate
    that drifts mid-set is absorbed frame by frame. smoothingSampleCount and
    observedSampleRateHz expose what it settled on.
    
    The window is held in primitive arrays rather than a deque of timestamped samples:
    same behaviour, but nothing is allocated per frame once the buffer has grown, which
    matters on the sensor callback.
    
    What it cost: minAmplitude 0.75 -> 0.94. The two Pixel traces are bit-identical
    before and after — 250 ms was already their filter. Hit's was 430 ms, and it is
    also where the amplitude band came from, so normalising it moved both edges: his
    ~63.5 s wobble rose 0.60 -> 0.83 and his softest rep 0.92 -> 1.03. Replaying all
    three traces puts the passing band at 0.87-1.02; 0.94 is its centre.
    
    The deeper cost, recorded in the KDoc: duration is no longer an independent second
    guard. At 430 ms the wobble ran 501 ms and minRepDurationMs rejected it unaided; at
    250 ms it runs 743 ms, longer than the shortest genuine rep in the corpus (615 ms).
    The populations have crossed over, so no minRepDurationMs can separate them and
    minAmplitude now carries the decision alone. The two ways back — a gravity estimate,
    so descent and ascent can be distinguished, or more participant data — are both real
    work, not a retune.
    
    Adds a test pinning the rate-invariance directly: a 250 ms window must hold 25/15/13
    samples at 100/58/50 Hz. Reverting the filter to a fixed count fails it with
    expected:<15> but was:<25>.
    
    Also corrects two stale comments in the synthetic-trace test. Its missing rep is the
    last, not the first: reps 1-9 are detected and the tenth cycle opens a window at
    ~19140 ms that the trace, ending at 19980 ms, never lets close — it is 9 reps at 20,
    100, 250 and 500 ms of smoothing, which rules out the warm-up the comment blamed.
    And the test's two parameter overrides stopped being required when the cooldown
    dropped to 500 ms; the defaults now give the same 9. They are left in place, since
    removing them changes what the test asserts.
    
    Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5 <noreply@anthropic.com>

commit 68b220b904cc88c644cdbdcb2733bf24ab16aef8
Author:     Mohit Nanda <mohit240903@gmail.com>
AuthorDate: Wed Sep 9 01:35:28 2026 +1000
Commit:     Mohit Nanda <mohit240903@gmail.com>
CommitDate: Wed Sep 9 01:35:28 2026 +1000

    feat(engine): support traces recorded in g as well as m/s^2
    
    Sensor Logger's iOS export has no TotalAcceleration.csv, and the file that comes
    closest — AccelerometerUncalibrated.csv — is written in multiples of gravity
    rather than m/s^2. Every threshold in SquatRepDetector sits around 9.81, so a
    trace in g would read as a phone barely moving and detect nothing.
    
    Adds a `units` key to the .expect format, defaulting to m/s^2 so existing traces
    are untouched, and has the loader multiply by 9.80665 on the way in. Declaring it
    per trace rather than sniffing it keeps the conversion explicit: a recording whose
    units are guessed wrong fails silently, and the whole point of the .expect file is
    that nothing about a fixture is implicit.
    
    Unknown unit names are rejected the same way unknown keys already are, so a typo
    cannot quietly fall back to m/s^2 and halve every reading.
    
    Note iOS's Accelerometer.csv is NOT usable here even though it is in m/s^2:
    gravity has already been removed, so a still phone reads ~0 and the thresholds
    have nothing to sit against.
    
    Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5 <noreply@anthropic.com>

commit 8d96bb36d7828dd669d162e9431f581262c2b856
Author:     Mohit Nanda <mohit240903@gmail.com>
AuthorDate: Sun Sep 6 21:21:18 2026 +1000
Commit:     Mohit Nanda <mohit240903@gmail.com>
CommitDate: Sun Sep 6 21:21:18 2026 +1000

    feat(sensors): add live SensorSource backed by SensorManager
    
    Adds the seam between real hardware and the engine: DeviceSensorSource registers
    TYPE_ACCELEROMETER and TYPE_GYROSCOPE at SENSOR_DELAY_GAME and emits a cold
    Flow<MotionFrame>, so the detector runs on a phone using the same code path the
    replay tests already exercise.
    
    The two sensors are never synchronised, so the listener stores the latest reading
    of each and a 50 Hz tick pairs them. A frame is only emitted once the accelerometer
    timestamp has advanced -- re-emitting a stale reading under a fresh timestamp would
    invent a flat stretch of signal exactly where the detector measures amplitude.
    
    TYPE_ACCELEROMETER is deliberate, not TYPE_LINEAR_ACCELERATION: the detector works
    on rotation-invariant magnitude, which needs gravity to park a still phone at ~9.81
    so the thresholds are absolute levels around a fixed baseline. Timestamps come from
    SensorEvent.timestamp (boot clock) rather than the wall clock, which can jump
    backwards mid-workout and hand a rep a negative duration.
    
    callbackFlow registers on collection and unregisters in awaitClose, so the sensors
    cannot outlive the workout. A missing gyroscope degrades to zeroed channels; a
    missing accelerometer completes an empty stream rather than fabricating readings.
    
    Adds kotlinx-coroutines-android, which the module did not depend on yet.
    
    Also corrects CLAUDE.md, which is relocated here from feature/engine-rep-detector
    now that it documents the Android module. Its package layout named a
    com.repmate.data.model package that does not exist -- the frozen models are in
    com.repmate.engine alongside RepDetector -- so code generated from it did not
    compile. The layout now separates packages that exist from ones still planned, and
    states the sensors/engine split: com.repmate.sensors is the only package allowed
    android.* imports, which is what keeps the engine's replay tests running on the JVM.
    
    Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5 <noreply@anthropic.com>
    Claude-Session: https://claude.ai/code/session_012T6EuRKn9c7vM7WpULWy5t

commit e2cdc0deab03ef4e0b7c79807eed13d80b8485b7
Author:     Mohit Nanda <mohit240903@gmail.com>
AuthorDate: Sun Sep 6 19:33:54 2026 +1000
Commit:     Mohit Nanda <mohit240903@gmail.com>
CommitDate: Sun Sep 6 19:33:54 2026 +1000

    feat(engine): integrate rep detector into the Android module
    
    Ports the standalone engine prototype into the Gradle project so it builds and
    tests as part of the app. Branched from main, so this merges cleanly -- unlike
    feature/engine-rep-detector, which carries the standalone project's unrelated
    history and its own .idea/, .iml and .gitignore at the repo root.
    
      src/*.kt  -> app/src/main/java/com/repmate/engine/   (package com.repmate.engine)
      test/*.kt -> app/src/test/java/com/repmate/engine/
      traces/   -> app/src/test/resources/traces/
    
    TraceLibrary now resolves its recordings from the classpath rather than by
    relative path. A unit test's working directory is Gradle's business, not the
    repository's, so the old relative lookup does not survive the move. It requires
    a file: URL -- what Gradle produces for unit-test resources -- and fails loudly
    otherwise, because a suite that silently discovers zero traces would pass every
    assertion while testing nothing. Adding a recording still needs no code change.
    
    The test dependency is kotlin-test-JUNIT, not plain kotlin-test. On the JVM
    kotlin.test.Test is an expect annotation whose actual is a typealias for
    org.junit.Test, and that lives in the framework-specific artifact. The Kotlin
    Gradle plugin selects it automatically; AGP's built-in Kotlin support does not,
    so testImplementation(kotlin("test")) resolves but fails to compile with
    "Unresolved reference 'Test'". Named explicitly via the version catalog.
    
    Verified on this branch: :app:assembleDebug succeeds, and :app:testDebugUnitTest
    runs 8 engine tests plus the existing ExampleUnitTest with 0 failures. Trace
    discovery was checked by breaking one ground truth and confirming the failure
    reads "1 of 3 trace(s)", so all three recordings are found from test resources
    rather than the suite quietly iterating an empty list.
    
    Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5 <noreply@anthropic.com>
    Claude-Session: https://claude.ai/code/session_01XrSnBoWMofZPduQtRbPocx

commit 240df197660f3c5e53a2f1a2bfb8b8778e566e82
Author:     Mohit Nanda <mohit240903@gmail.com>
AuthorDate: Sun Sep 6 18:56:00 2026 +1000
Commit:     Mohit Nanda <mohit240903@gmail.com>
CommitDate: Sun Sep 6 18:56:00 2026 +1000

    fix(engine): centre minRepDurationMs in its band at 550 ms
    
    600 ms passed every trace but sat 15 ms below the shortest real rep (615 ms),
    and 620 ms already discards that rep. A squat a few per cent quicker than the
    fastest one recorded would have been dropped silently, and a miscount is the
    failure a user notices most.
    
    550 ms sits roughly centred in the 501-615 ms band instead: 49 ms above the
    teammate's wobble and 65 ms below the shortest genuine rep. Same guard, same
    job, positioned so that neither edge is close.
    
    All three traces still detect 10 of 10 in their set windows, with unchanged
    whole-recording counts of 12, 13 and 12.
    
    Also corrects a wrong note in squat_10_pocket.expect: its 615 ms rep caps
    minRepDurationMs from above, it does not set a lower bound.

commit 414ba79f250db4deaaed03c74c377ddcb0a0da1d
Author:     Mohit Nanda <mohit240903@gmail.com>
AuthorDate: Sun Sep 6 18:53:10 2026 +1000
Commit:     Mohit Nanda <mohit240903@gmail.com>
CommitDate: Sun Sep 6 18:53:10 2026 +1000

    fix(engine): separate the cooldown's two jobs with an amplitude guard
    
    A fast set (~1.9 s per rep) exposed that cooldownMs was silently doing two jobs:
    suppressing the rebound of a rep already counted, and -- as a side effect of
    being 2000 ms long -- swallowing small wobbles that no other guard rejected. At
    that pace the cooldown discarded genuine reps beginning under 2 s after the
    previous one ended, finding 5 of 10.
    
    No single cooldown value fixes this. The fast set needs 900 ms or less; a second
    participant's trace needs 1000 ms or more to keep a 0.60-amplitude, 501 ms wobble
    out. Those requirements do not overlap, so retuning the number was never going to
    work -- 1000 ms only appears to fix the fast set because its whole-recording
    count reaches 10 as two real reps are lost and two handling artifacts counted.
    
    The fix is to split the jobs. minAmplitude rejects wobbles on their own merits,
    which frees cooldownMs to be only what its name says:
    
      minAmplitude     0.75  (new)  between a 0.60 non-rep and a 0.92 real rep
      minRepDurationMs 600   (500)  between a 501 ms wobble and a 615 ms real rep
      cooldownMs       500  (2000)  headroom above the fastest real gap of 917 ms
    
    Duration is deliberately kept as a second independent guard rather than relying
    on amplitude alone: the amplitude floor rests on a single participant's softest
    rep, and amplitude is the most person-dependent quantity here, ranging 3.2x
    across three people. The wobble fails both guards, so neither has to carry the
    decision by itself -- each has an uncomfortable margin alone (15 ms for duration,
    0.32 m/s^2 for amplitude) and together they do not.
    
    All three traces now detect 10 of 10 in their set windows, up from 10/10/5.
    squat_10_fast.expect records fullTraceEvents 12 (was 7 under the old cooldown).
    
    Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5 <noreply@anthropic.com>
    Claude-Session: https://claude.ai/code/session_01XrSnBoWMofZPduQtRbPocx

    test(engine): add second participant trace as a validation fixture
    
    10 squats recorded by a different person on a Samsung SM-S936B at ~58 Hz, right
    front pocket, at a faster ~3 s cadence than the baseline recording. This is the
    first trace from someone other than the person the detector was tuned on.
    
    The production thresholds (10.15 / 9.7 / 500 ms / 2000 ms) pass unchanged:
    10 reps inside the 55000-102000 ms set window, and 13 events across the whole
    recording -- the 10 squats plus three handling artifacts, two before the set and
    one after. The 15000-50000 ms stretch where they stood still yields nothing,
    making it a false-positive check on real data rather than a synthetic one.
    
    Nothing was retuned to accommodate this trace.
    
    Two caveats worth recording. The 25-sample smoothing window is specified in
    samples, not milliseconds, so at 58 Hz it spans ~430 ms rather than the ~250 ms
    documented at 100 Hz; it survived that 2x rate change rather than being correct
    by design. And the tightest gap between reps here is 2.82 s, so the 2000 ms
    cooldown had headroom and was not genuinely stressed -- a set faster than ~2 s
    between reps is still untested.

commit b35ae38183470929f16dbbb2c93a2d981937396b
Author:     Mohit Nanda <mohit240903@gmail.com>
AuthorDate: Sun Sep 6 16:55:36 2026 +1000
Commit:     Mohit Nanda <mohit240903@gmail.com>
CommitDate: Sun Sep 6 16:55:36 2026 +1000

    refactor(engine): support multiple traces with per-trace ground-truth manifests
    
    Recordings now live in traces/, each with a companion .expect manifest declaring
    its exercise, ground-truth rep count, the time window of the actual set, and an
    optional quiet window. The test suite iterates the trace library rather than
    hard-coding one file, so adding a recording needs no test changes -- drop in a
    .csv and its .expect and every assertion picks it up.
    
    The set window is what makes the count honest: a recording starts before the
    phone is in the pocket and stops after it is back out, so the raw file holds
    movement bursts that are not reps. squat_10_pocket now asserts both halves of
    that story separately -- exactly 10 reps inside 12000-54000 ms, and 12 events
    across the whole file, the 10 squats plus the two handling artifacts.
    
    Two deliberate strictnesses, both so a fixture cannot quietly stop asserting: a
    .csv with no companion .expect is an error rather than a skip, and an unknown
    key in a manifest is rejected, since a typo'd key would otherwise switch off an
    assertion while the suite stayed green.
    
    Main.kt iterates the library too, marking events that fall outside the set
    window, so a new recording can be eyeballed as soon as it is dropped in.
    
    Suite goes from 5 tests to 8; all pass.
    
    Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5 <noreply@anthropic.com>
    Claude-Session: https://claude.ai/code/session_01XrSnBoWMofZPduQtRbPocx

commit e75ae79ad8f5e972fea8abe19c63a00e626bd7de
Author:     Mohit Nanda <mohit240903@gmail.com>
AuthorDate: Sun Sep 6 16:29:12 2026 +1000
Commit:     Mohit Nanda <mohit240903@gmail.com>
CommitDate: Sun Sep 6 16:29:41 2026 +1000

    feat(engine): detect reps from acceleration magnitude with smoothing and guards
    
    Switched from single-axis ay to orientation-invariant acceleration magnitude,
    so the detector no longer assumes the phone is held upright: gravity leaks
    across all three axes at an unpredictable pocket angle, and sqrt(ax^2+ay^2+az^2)
    reads the same at any orientation.
    
    Added a 25-sample moving-average filter to flatten footfall shock and pocket
    rustle, and a Schmitt-trigger state machine with minimum-duration and cooldown
    guards to reject fidgets and post-rep rebound.
    
    Thresholds tuned against a real pocket recording (10 squats, ~99 Hz), loaded
    via a Sensor Logger CSV parser that resolves columns by header name -- the
    export orders its axes z,y,x, so positional parsing would silently swap them.
    
    Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5 <noreply@anthropic.com>
    Claude-Session: https://claude.ai/code/session_01XrSnBoWMofZPduQtRbPocx

commit 80bc9b7aef56e3c0d17dda704dba17d2fbeb1f86
Author:     Mohit Nanda <mohit240903@gmail.com>
AuthorDate: Sun Aug 23 22:56:58 2026 +1000
Commit:     Mohit Nanda <mohit240903@gmail.com>
CommitDate: Sun Aug 23 22:56:58 2026 +1000

    Add motion models, synthetic squat trace, and rep detector
    
    Initial commit of the repmate engine core, pure Kotlin with no Android
    dependencies:
    
    - Models.kt: MotionFrame, RepEvent, RepScore, WorkoutSession, plus the
      ExerciseType and RepPhase enums.
    - SyntheticTrace.kt: deterministic squat trace generator with a known rep
      count, so the detector can be developed against ground truth before real
      phone data exists.
    - RepDetector.kt: SquatRepDetector counts reps from vertical acceleration
      using an IDLE -> DESCENDING -> ASCENDING -> IDLE state machine driven by
      configurable down/up thresholds around gravity. No false-positive guards
      yet.
    
    Detects 10/10 reps on a 10-rep synthetic trace, including when the trace is
    fed in across multiple processAll calls.
    
    Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5 <noreply@anthropic.com>
